# How this repository was made (Sardinia)

This file is written by the build on 2026-10-30, the day the source data was retrieved.

## Sources

| Source | Address | Retrieved | Licence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Regione Autonoma della Sardegna, Banca dati giuridica delle leggi regionali | <https://leggiregionali.regione.sardegna.it/> | 2026-10-30 | Legge 633/1941, art. 5 (official acts are not protected); site terms (www.regione.sardegna.it/note-legali): use for non-commercial purposes with the source "Regione Sardegna" and its URL cited. |

Every word of law in this repository comes from these sources. Lexifina reads them and records each act's dated versions, and the laws that made each change, in one corpus file. This repository is built from that file.

Other counts:

## Dates

Each commit is dated at noon (Europe/Rome) on the day the change took effect. The other dates, enacted and published, are trailers.

When a law gives no date of its own, the default rule applies: Statuto speciale per la Sardegna (legge costituzionale 26 febbraio 1948, n. 3), art. 33: a regional law enters into force on the fifteenth day after its publication in the Bollettino Ufficiale, unless the law sets another term. https://www.normattiva.it/uri-res/N2Ls?urn:nir:stato:legge.costituzionale:1948-02-26;3

## How the history is built

`tools/build.py` turns the corpus into this history in one pass: one commit per law, on the day it took effect. The same corpus always gives the same commit hashes. `tools/check_repo.py` reads every version back from git and compares it with the corpus. `tools/on.py` shows the law as it stood on any date. The corpus format is described in `tools/SCHEMA.md`.

- Corpus: sha256 `0fc270d169230ab5498b5bad19b28e7aaa4b43aea5343edbc55bc196aa89a0f3` (22022939 bytes).
- Builder blob hashes (`git hash-object`): `build.py` `a4faa25af85facb64b956b372f6b528aafdd7ec1`, `check.py` `3d00541d258745461ab52b1b0cb4e1716a27a95d`, `on.py` `644a6c0857fbcc72562032b58d57aa113fee0641`.
- Python 3.12.10; git version 2.46.0.windows.1.

## Checks

1755 versions of 1755 acts read back from git on the day each took effect and on the day before: 0 mismatches. Trailers: 0 commits whose trailers git cannot parse. Health: 0 merge commits on main, 0 commit dates that go backwards, 0 commits dated after the build.

No second build of this corpus has been compared yet.

## Decisions made by hand

- Current-only: each act is the current text the Banca dati giuridica publishes for the law, as one "oldest version available" commit dated the retrieval day (the database has no dated past versions).
- Text is read from each PDF's text layer (PyMuPDF): lines of the body font are joined into paragraphs by their vertical spacing; across a page break a line ending in ".", ";" or ":" ends its paragraph. Superscript note markers and the editor's notes after the body are left out.
- Units: intestazione (title, publication line, promulgation formula) and one file per "Art." heading; other centred headings (Titolo, Capo, the article's rubric) go in the file of the article they stand in or before. A repeated article number gets the suffix -dupN.
- Laws the database marks as repealed in full ("* Legge abrogata ..." under the title) are left out; every other law in the database is included, budget and accounts laws too, except the 308 laws whose PDF the database generates from older XML/SGML records (catalogue fileType other than "AKN"): those print the editor notes inline in the body font and are left out until a reader separates them.

## What AI did and did not do

The scripts that read the sources and build this repository were written by Claude Code running Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) under Lexifina's direction.

No model wrote, translated or corrected legal text. No model runs at build time. Every word of law in this repository comes from the official sources above.

The commit messages are written by `build.py` from the source data.

Model use at data-build time:
- A model (Claude) wrote the fetch and parse code and chose the layout; it wrote no law text. Every word of law comes from the Region's PDFs.
